# Excalidraw Data

## Text Elements
∫ f(x) dx = F(x), not f(x)²/2 (that's only true for f(x)=x).
In y' = x·y², f(x) = x  →  ∫ x dx = x²/2 ^LQgFkCpw

Simpler rule: ln only for power -1.
1/y   = y^(-1)  →  ln|y|
1/y² = y^(-2)  →  power rule: y^(-2+1)/(-2+1) = y^(-1)/(-1) = -1/y ^xm6EiUUb

Right idea, wrong letter: the ln rule is for (variable)^(-1), i.e. y^(-1).
g(y)^(-1) is NOT y^(-1): with g(y)=y², g(y)^(-1) = (y²)^(-1) = y^(-2) → power rule → -1/y.
ln|g(y)| only works if g'(y) sits on top (chain rule). ^0U034oyH

g(y) is a placeholder, like f(x): you can't integrate a letter.
General recipe stays as  ∫ dy/g(y) = ∫ f(x) dx  (written, not computed).
In an exercise: replace g with the real expression (y², 1+y, e^y...)
then integrate it with the usual tools (power rule, fractions, substitution). ^gI1exjQB
